# Assign an Activity — the runbook

The repeatable path for putting an LSAT U Activity into a student's hands.
Written 2026-09-07 after walking it live on lsatprep.study.

Joshua says **"assign &lt;activity&gt; to &lt;student&gt;"** and this is what happens,
end to end, with no further questions in the normal case.

Companion: [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]] — which platform account is which
student. Read it before ticking a name.

## Standing authorization

Joshua granted this on 2026-09-07: assigning an Activity to a **named student**
is a **go** — do it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.

This is a deliberate exception to the global "draft, never send" rule, which
still holds for email, posts, and messages. It exists because assignment is the
core loop of the tutoring business and confirming each one made it useless.

**Still confirm first when:**

- assigning to **3 or more students at once** (each one emails a real person)
- the name is **ambiguous** — most importantly **Steven Morris vs "Stevie"**,
  two accounts that may be one person
- the student is on the unresolved list: **Thaniuska Vivas** (no account),
  **Amanda** (no account found)
- Joshua names a student who is **not in the identity map at all**

## The path

The Activity Centre lives at `/activities` — Classroom sidebar → **Study** →
**Activities**. Joshua's account is admin, so each card in **The library** carries
its own assign button.

1. Navigate to `https://lsatprep.study/activities`
2. `find` the button **"Assign &lt;Activity name&gt;"** (one per card, exact title)
3. Click it — a dialog opens, titled **"Assign: &lt;Activity name&gt;"**
4. Optional: fill **"Schedule for"** (a date input) if Joshua named a due date
5. `read_page` the dialog and locate the student's checkbox by display name
6. Click the checkbox — the footer button updates to **"Assign to 1 students"**
7. Click that button
8. Confirm the result, then report what was sent

The dialog is a portal — it renders **outside `<main>`**, so `get_page_text` will
not show it. Use `read_page` (with the dialog's `ref_id` to keep output small).

## The nine Activities

Assign by the exact card title:

| # | World | Activity | Grain |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | The Specimen Lab | Vocab Rehab | a word |
| 02 | The Signal Observatory | Spot the Relationship | a sentence |
| 03 | The Switchyard | Conditional Rephrase | a statement, translated |
| 04 | The Polarity Chamber | Negation Station | a statement, negated |
| 05 | The Argument Atelier | Find the Conclusion | an argument |
| 06 | The Debate Chamber | Rebuttal Rephrase | an argument, restated |
| 07 | The Press | Dumb it Down | a paragraph |
| 08 | The Loom | Spot the Callback | a passage |
| 09 | Out Loud | Read, Recall, Colour | a whole section, against the clock |

### Read, Recall, Colour

Assign **card 09** — the section-library door. The student then chooses any of
the 12 seeded sections, each 25 minutes:

PT1.S3 · PT21.S3 · PT101.S2 · PT102.S3 · PT103.S1 · PT104.S1 · PT105.S1 ·
PT105.S2 · PT106.S2 · PT112.S4 · PT134.S3 · PT140.S1

Every attempt is kept. Re-running a section saves a **new attempt**; it never
replaces the old one.

Only assign an individual `read-recall-colour-pt*-s*` slug when Joshua
deliberately wants her pinned to one section. The door is the default.

## What assigning actually does

`assignWork()` — `projects/lsatu/src/lib/assignWork.ts`:

1. **Snapshots** the Activity into `activity_assignments`. The snapshot is
   immutable once issued, so later content edits do not reach an assignment
   already sent.
2. **Writes a `classroom_items` row** — this is what makes it reachable at all.
3. **Notifies on four channels**: inbox message, bell, email, classroom item.

Notification failure never fails the assignment. A student can therefore hold an
assignment she was never told about — worth checking if she says she got nothing.

# Assign work on LSAT U

Put an Activity, a guided drill, or a message into a student's LSAT U account
from the command line. No browser, no clicking.

## Authorization

Joshua pre-authorized this on 2026-09-07: **a single named student is a go.**
Run it, then report. Do not stop to ask permission first.

Confirm before running when:

- assigning to **3 or more students at once** — each one emails a real person
- the student name is **ambiguous** (the script refuses and lists the matches)
- the student is **Thaniuska Vivas** or **Amanda** — no platform account found
- Joshua names someone absent from [[STUDENT-PLATFORM-IDENTITY-MAP]]

This is a deliberate, narrow exception to his global "draft, never send" rule.
That rule still holds for email, posts, and anything outside LSAT U.

## The command

Run from `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu`:

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
```

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Lydia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
```

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Dakota" --message "Start with PT105.S2 before Thursday."
```

Flags: `--schedule YYYY-MM-DD` unlocks the work that morning. `--dry-run`
resolves the student and the work and writes nothing — use it when the name is
at all uncertain.

To pace a student over several days, run the command once per day with a
`--schedule` on each. Each run is its own assignment, its own row, and its own
email, so she is told about each day rather than handed a block.

Every assignment lands on the home calendar strip and the cell opens that
assignment — scheduled work on its unlock day, unscheduled work on the day you
assigned it. Nothing needs to be written by hand to put work on the calendar;
if an assignment is missing from the band, that is a bug to fix in
`useClassroomHome`, not something to patch into `classroom_items`.

Always `--dry-run` first for a student you have not assigned to before in this
session. It costs one second and it is the only cheap way to catch a wrong
account.

## Why a script and not a direct DB write

`scripts/assign.ts` calls the app's **own** `assignWork()` from
`src/lib/assignWork.ts`. Assigning touches seven tables — the assignment
snapshot, `classroom_items`, the conversation and its participants, `messages`,
`notifications` — plus the email edge function, and it rolls the whole thing
back if the classroom item or notify throws. There is no RPC and no edge
function for it; it is client-side code.

So never hand-write these rows. A second implementation drifts from the UI the
first time the app changes, and produces assignments subtly unlike the ones
Joshua makes by clicking.

## Credentials

Read from `~/ai-os/.env`, which is **not** a git repo:

- `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL`, `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` — already set

Never put a credential in `projects/lsatu/.env`. That file **is tracked in git**
and would be committed. The script signs in as Joshua and works under normal
admin RLS, the same permission path the UI uses.

`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is deliberately **not** used even when present: it
bypasses RLS on every write, so a script running as service_role would hide an
RLS regression instead of hitting it. The script warns and falls back.

## Expected output

```
Assign "Read, Recall, Colour" → Celia <celiacutler@gmail.com>
Assigned. id=e83e2193-…
  inbox ok · bell ok · email ok
```

A notify channel reading `FAILED` is **not** an assignment failure — the work is
in her Classroom and only a mess...

import { useCallback, useEffect, useState } from "react";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { useClassId } from "@/hooks/useClassId";

/**
 * Admin-scoped CRUD for homework_sets.
 * RLS enforces created_by = auth.uid() and has_role(..., 'admin').
 */

export type InstructionLayout = 'two_column' | 'single_column';

export interface HomeworkSet {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  description: string | null;
  question_qids: string[];
  created_by: string;
  class_id: string;
  created_at: string;
  updated_at: string;
  /** Guided-set extension fields (nullable on legacy rows). */
  is_guided_set?: boolean;
  instructions_markdown?: string | null;
  instruction_layout?: InstructionLayout | null;
  guided_set_type?: string | null;
  question_type?: string | null;
  reasoning_type?: string | null;
  question_feature?: string | null;
  estimated_minutes?: number | null;
  source_pts?: string[] | null;
  difficulty_min?: number | null;
  difficulty_max?: number | null;
  start_button_label?: string | null;
}

export interface GuidedSetFields {
  is_guided_set?: boolean;
  instructions_markdown?: string | null;
  instruction_layout?: InstructionLayout | null;
  guided_set_type?: string | null;
  question_type?: string | null;
  reasoning_type?: string | null;
  question_feature?: string | null;
  estimated_minutes?: number | null;
  source_pts?: string[] | null;
  difficulty_min?: number | null;
  difficulty_max?: number | null;
  start_button_label?: string | null;
}

export interface CreateHomeworkSetInput extends GuidedSetFields {
  title: string;
  description?: string | null;
  question_qids: string[];
}

export interface UpdateHomeworkSetInput extends GuidedSetFields {
  title?: string;
  description?: string | null;
  question_qids?: string[];
}

export interface MutationResult<T> {
  data: T | null;
  error: string | null;
}

export interface UseHomeworkSetsResult {
  sets: HomeworkSet[];
  loading: boolean;
  error: string | null;
  classIdReady: boolean;
  refresh: () => Promise<void>;
  create: (input: CreateHomeworkSetInput) => Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>>;
  update: (id: string, input: UpdateHomeworkSetInput) => Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>>;
  remove: (id: string) => Promise<{ ok: boolean; error: string | null }>;
}

export function useHomeworkSets(): UseHomeworkSetsResult {

async function signIn(): Promise<string> {
  if (env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) {
    console.warn(
      "! SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is set but this script signs in as the admin user.\n" +
        "  Using it would require injecting a second client into assignWork, which would\n" +
        "  bypass RLS for every write. Falling back to the admin login.",
    );
  }
  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;
  const password = [REDACTED];
  if (!email || !password) {
    console.error(
      "assign: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set.\n" +
        "  Add them to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). This repo's .env is TRACKED in git,\n" +
        "  so a credential placed there would be committed.",
    );
    process.exit(2);
  }
  const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });
  if (error || !data.user) {
    console.error(`assign: sign-in failed — ${error?.message ?? "no user returned"}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return data.user.id;
}

// -------------------------------------------------------------- resolution

interface StudentRow {
  class_id: string;
  display_name: string | null;
  email: string | null;
  [k: string]: unknown;
}

/**
 * Students come from the admin-manage-users edge function, which cross-joins
 * profiles with auth.users for the email. `class_id` is the canonical student
 * identifier everywhere in this codebase.
 */
async function listStudents(): Promise<StudentRow[]> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);
  const rows = (data ?? []) as StudentRow[];
  if (!Array.isArray(rows)) throw new Error("admin-manage-users returned no user list");
  return rows;
}

/**
 * Resolve a name or email to exactly one student. Ambiguity is fatal rather
 * than resolved by picking the first: sending a student's homework to the wrong
 * account is silent — she simply never sees it, and he never learns why.
 */
function resolveStudent(rows: StudentRow[], query: string): StudentRow {
  const q = query.trim().toLowerCase();
  const exact = rows.filter(
    (r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q,
  );
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows.filter(
    (r) =>
      r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q),
  );

  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no student matches "${query}".`);
    console.error("Known:", rows.map((r) => r.display_name ?? r.email).join(" · "));
    process.exit(1);
  }
  if (pool.length > 1) {
    console.error(`assign: "${query}" is ambiguous — ${pool.length} accounts match:`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.display_name ?? "(no name)"}  <${r.email ?? "?"}>`);
    console.error("Narrow it with the full name or the email address.");
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return pool[0];
}

async function resolveActivity(query: string) {
  const q = query.trim();
  const bySlug = await supabase.from("activities").select("id,title,slug,mode").eq("slug", q).maybeSingle();
  if (bySlug.data) return bySlug.data;

  const { data, error } = await supabase
    .from("activities")
    .select("id,title,slug,mode")
    .ilike("title", `%${q}%`);
  if (error) throw error;
  const rows = data ?? [];

/** Mirrors findOrCreateThread in src/lib/classroomNotify.ts, which is private. */
async function findOrCreateThread(adminUserId: string, studentId: string): Promise<string> {
  const { data: mine } = await supabase
    .from("conversation_participants")
    .select("conversation_id")
    .eq("user_id", adminUserId);
  const { data: theirs } = await supabase
    .from("conversation_participants")
    .select("conversation_id")
    .eq("user_id", studentId);

  const ids = new Set((mine ?? []).map((r) => r.conversation_id));
  const shared = (theirs ?? []).find((r) => ids.has(r.conversation_id));
  if (shared) return shared.conversation_id as string;

  const { data: conv, error } = await supabase
    .from("conversations")
    .insert({})
    .select("id")
    .single();
  if (error || !conv) throw error ?? new Error("could not create conversation");

  const { error: pErr } = await supabase.from("conversation_participants").insert([
    { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: adminUserId },
    { conversation_id: conv.id, user_id: studentId },
  ]);
  if (pErr) throw pErr;
  return conv.id as string;
}

/**
 * The flag that has to be on for a student to open what she was just assigned.
 *
 * Every route into this work sits behind a `ProtectedRoute`, and Classroom's
 * own feed drops rows a student cannot open, so a missing flag does not read as
 * a locked door: the work is simply invisible, and the link Joshua sends says
 * "Not open yet".
 *
 * That happened. Ivory Johnson was assigned three Read, Recall, Colour sections
 * on 2026-09-08 with `has_activity_access` off. He mailed Joshua on the Monday
 * to say the portal was empty, tried the link on the Tuesday, was told it was
 * not in his plan, and lost a day of study to a boolean. Nothing in the assign
 * path had any opinion about whether he could reach what it was writing.
 *
 * Assigning work he cannot open is never what Joshua means, so this grants the
 * one flag the assigned work needs. It never grants anything else and it never
 * revokes: the rest of the feature set stays exactly where he set it.
 */
const ACCESS_FLAG = {
  activity: "has_activity_access",
  guided_drill: "has_drill_access",
} as const;

async function ensureCanOpen(classId: string, kind: keyof typeof ACCESS_FLAG, who: string) {
  const flag = ACCESS_FLAG[kind];

    .from("profiles")
    .select(flag)
    .eq("class_id", classId)
    .single();
  if (error) {
    console.warn(`! Could not check ${flag} for ${who}: ${error.message}`);
    console.warn("  The work is assigned. Confirm they can open it before telling them it is there.");
    return;

export function useHomeworkSets(): UseHomeworkSetsResult {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const { classId, loading: classIdLoading } = useClassId();
  const [sets, setSets] = useState<HomeworkSet[]>([]);
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {
    // Returning while `loading` is still true left the list stuck on its
    // skeleton forever for anyone signed out — a spinner that never resolves
    // reads as a broken page, not as "nothing to show."
    if (!user) {
      setLoading(false);
      return;
    }
    setLoading(true);
    const { data, error: err } = await supabase
      .from("homework_sets")
      .select("*")
      .eq("created_by", user.id)
      .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
    if (err) {
      console.error("[homework:sets:list] failed", { userId: user.id, error: err });
      setError(err.message);
      setSets([]);
    } else {
      setSets((data || []) as HomeworkSet[]);
      setError(null);
    }
    setLoading(false);
  }, [user]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (user) void refresh();
  }, [user, refresh]);

  const create = useCallback(
    async (input: CreateHomeworkSetInput): Promise<MutationResult<HomeworkSet>> => {
      if (!user) return { data: null, error: "Not signed in." };
      if (classIdLoading) return { data: null, error: "Still loading your class. Try again in a moment." };
      const effectiveClassId = classId || user.id;
      const guidedFields: Record<string, unknown> = {};
      const guidedKeys: (keyof GuidedSetFields)[] = [
        'is_guided_set', 'instructions_markdown', 'instruction_layout',
        'guided_set_type', 'question_type', 'reasoning_type', 'question_feature',
        'estimated_minutes', 'source_pts', 'difficulty_min', 'difficulty_max',
        'start_button_label',
      ];
      for (const k of guidedKeys) {
        const v = input[k];
        if (v !== undefined) guidedFields[k] = v;
      }
      // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
      const { data, error: err } = await supabase
        .from("homework_sets")
        .insert({
          title: input.title,
          description: input.description ?? null,
          question_qids: input.question_qids,
          created_by: user.id,
          class_id: effectiveClassId,
[/external_agent_tool_result]

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS homework_assignments_student_status_idx
  ON public.homework_assignments (student_id, status, created_at DESC);

-- At most one active assignment per (student, set). A completed assignment
-- does not block re-issuing the same set.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS homework_assignments_active_unique
  ON public.homework_assignments (student_id, set_id)
  WHERE status <> 'completed' AND set_id IS NOT NULL;
[/external_agent_tool_result]

# section + set resolvers, inserted before messaging section
s=s.replace('''// ---------------------------------------------------------------- messaging
''','''// ------------------------------------------------------- PT sections / sets

/**
 * A timed PT section is a homework set whose qids are exactly one section of
 * one PrepTest. `drillRunSpec.singleSectionOf()` notices that shape and runs
 * it as a real 35-minute `full-section` instead of an untimed drill, so the
 * qids have to come from the section file itself, in order, with nothing
 * else mixed in. The file under public/data is the same one the section
 * runner loads, so the set can never name a question the runner lacks.
 */
const SECTION_PATTERN = /^PT\\s*(\\d+)[.\\-\\s]*S(?:ection)?\\s*(\\d+)$/i;

function sectionTitle(pt: number, section: number) {
  return `PT${pt}.S${section} (LR)`;
}

function readSectionQids(query: string): { pt: number; section: number; qids: string[] } {
  const m = SECTION_PATTERN.exec(query.trim());
  if (!m) {
    console.error(`assign: --section wants PT<n>.S<n> (e.g. PT156.S2), got "${query}".`);
    process.exit(2);
  }
  const pt = Number(m[1]);
  const section = Number(m[2]);
  const file = path.join(REPO, "public", "data", `PT${pt}-S${section}-LR.json`);
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) {
    const have = fs
      .readdirSync(path.join(REPO, "public", "data"))
      .filter((f) => f.startsWith(`PT${pt}-`) && f.endsWith("-LR.json"))
      .map((f) => f.replace(/-LR\\.json$/, "").replace("-", "."));
    console.error(
      `assign: no LR section file for PT${pt}.S${section}.` +
        (have.length ? ` PT${pt} has: ${have.join(", ")}.` : ` Nothing for PT${pt} is loaded.`),
    );
    process.exit(1);
  }
  const rows = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8")) as Array<{
    practiceTest: string;
    sectionNumber: number;
    questionNumber: number;
  }>;
  const qids = rows
    .filter((r) => r.practiceTest === `PT${pt}` && r.sectionNumber === section)
    .sort((a, b) => a.questionNumber - b.questionNumber)
    .map((r) => `PT${pt}-S${section}-Q${r.questionNumber}`);
  if (qids.length < 20) {
    console.error(`assign: ${file} holds ${qids.length} questions; a full LR section has 24–26.`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return { pt, section, qids };
}

/**
 * One set per section, reused. Two sets with the same title would make the
 * teacher UI show duplicates and the unique index on (student, set) useless.
 */
async function findOrCreateSectionSet(teacherId: string, query: string, write: boolean) {
  const { pt, section, qids } = readSectionQids(query);
  const title = sectionTitle(pt, section);
  const { data: existing, error } = await supabase
    .from("homework_sets")
    .select("id,title,question_qids")
    .eq("title", title)
    .order("created_at", { ascending: true });
  if (error) throw error;
  const match = (existing ?? []).find(
    (s) => JSON.stringify(s.question_qids ?? []) === JSON.stringify(qids),
  );
  if (match) return { id: match.id, title: match.title, created: false, count: qids.length };
  if (existing?.length) {
    console.error(
      `assign: a set titled "${title}" exists but its questions differ from ${path.basename(
        `PT${pt}-S${section}-LR.json`,
      )}. Fix the set in Teacher → Homework before assigning.`,
    );
    process.exit(1);
  }
  if (!write) return { id: "(new)", title, created: true, count: qids.length };
  const { data, error: insErr } = await supabase
    .from("homework_sets")
    .insert({
      title,
      description: `PrepTest ${pt}, Section ${section} — Logical Reasoning. Standard time.`,
      question_qids: qids,
      created_by: teacherId,
      class_id: teacherId,
      estimated_minutes: 35,
    })
    .select("id,title")
    .single();
  if (insErr) throw insErr;
  return { id: data.id, title: data.title, created: true, count: qids.length };
}

async function resolveSet(query: string) {
  const q = query.trim();
  if (/^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/i.test(q)) {
    const { data } = await supabase.from("homework_sets").select("id,title").eq("id", q).maybeSingle();
    if (data) return data;
  }
  const { data, error } = await supabase.from("homework_sets").select("id,title").ilike("title", `%${q}%`);
  if (error) throw error;
  const rows = data ?? [];
  const exact = rows.filter((r) => r.title.toLowerCase() === q.toLowerCase());
  const pool = exact.length ? exact : rows;
  if (pool.length === 0) {
    console.error(`assign: no homework set matches "${query}".`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  if (pool.length > 1) {
    console.error(`assign: "${query}" matches ${pool.length} sets:`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.title}  (${r.id})`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return pool[0];
}

/** Due at the end of that calendar day, local time — "due Saturday" means all of Saturday. */
function dueAtEndOfDay(day: string): string {
  const [y, m, d] = day.split("-").map(Number);
  return new Date(y, m - 1, d, 23, 59, 59).toISOString();
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- messaging

Section Time Remaining: 34:59

When primatologist Akira Suzuki began studying snow monkeys in the 1950s, he found that they often roamed out of the mountains to feed in apple orchards. After a decade of observing this behavior, Suzuki began to feed the monkeys in their mountain habitat by providing them with soybeans to eat. The monkeys no longer raided the orchards. When Suzuki began his work, 23 snow monkeys lived in the region he studied. The population today is 270 snow monkeys and is expected to continue growing.

1.

A
Snow monkeys do not feed outside of their mountain habitat when food is readily available within it.
B
For snow monkeys, soybeans provide more complete nutrition than

description: Backfill estimated_minutes on reuse; re-run (expect duplicate guard after backfill)
command: python - "scripts/assign.ts" <<'EOF'
import sys
p=sys.argv[1]; s=open(p,encoding='utf8').read()
old='''    .select("id,title,question_qids")
    .eq("title", title)
    .order("created_at", { ascending: true });
  if (error) throw error;
  const match = (existing ?? []).find(
    (s) => JSON.stringify(s.question_qids ?? []) === JSON.stringify(qids),
  );
  if (match) return { id: match.id, title: match.title, created: false, count: qids.length };'''
new='''    .select("id,title,question_qids,estimated_minutes")
    .eq("title", title)
    .order("created_at", { ascending: true });
  if (error) throw error;
  const match = (existing ?? []).find(
    (s) => JSON.stringify(s.question_qids ?? []) === JSON.stringify(qids),
  );
  if (match) {
    // The classroom card reads this; without it she is told "about 50
    // minutes" for a 35-minute section.
    if (match.estimated_minutes !== 35 && write) {
      await supabase.from("homework_sets").update({ estimated_minutes: 35 }).eq("id", match.id);
    }
    return { id: match.id, title: match.title, created: false, count: qids.length };
  }'''
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new,1)
open(p,'w',encoding='utf8',newline='\n').write(s); print("ok")

function PracticeTestSelector({
  tests,

}: {
  tests: PracticeTestBlueprint[];
  onBegin: (
    blueprint: PracticeTestBlueprint,
    pace: PracticeTestPaceId,
    sectionSeconds: number | null,

  const [pt, setPt] = React.useState<number | null>(null);
  const [pace, setPace] = React.useState<PracticeTestPaceId>("standard");
  const { settings } = useUserSettings();

   * The student's own standard clock, the same reading the section door takes
   * (SectionSelector's baseMinutes). Every pace here is a percentage of this,
   * so "Standard" is their normal section rather than a flat 35 minutes. This
   * door ignored the setting entirely before, which meant the same two words
   * named two different clocks in one room.
          </SelectContent>
        </Select>
        {/* The section door has carried this since its timing control was
            built. Without it this door offered accommodations while saying
            nothing about the standing setting those percentages are taken
            from, on the longer of the two commitments. */}

          <AccommodationNote baseMinutes={baseMinutes} />
        </p>

                <b>Section {i + 1}</b>
                <span>{legLabel(leg as never)}</span>
                <em>
                  {leg.questionCount} questions
                  {clock ? ` · ${clock}` : ""}
                </em>
              </div>
            ))}
          </div>
          <p className="testing-note">
            {chosen.questionCount} scored questions, with a ten-minute break
            after the second section.
          </p>
          {/* The clock is read from the chosen pace rather than printed as
              35:00. It was hard-coded, which stopped being true the moment this
              door started honouring the student's own timing mode: an
              accommodated student saw 52:30 on the control and "the real 35:00"
              in the paragraph under it. The clause drops out entirely when
              there is no clock, rather than asserting a number for a sitting
              that is not being timed. */}
          {!chosen.rcFullLength && (

              The Reading Comprehension section on this PrepTest runs a few

**Q1 — Where does "Open the set" land?**

The LawHub page has two halves: the left half is a whole-PrepTest view (title + a pill per section, each with its own status), and the right half is the intro for the section you picked. For a one-section assignment, which do you want?

- **A. Right half only (suggested).** The page is the section intro: "The Official LSAT PrepTest 143", "Section 1", "Questions 25", Begin, the official LR directions. No sibling-section pills, since she was assigned one section and the others would be either dead pills or a way to wander off the assignment.
- **B. Both halves, LawHub-faithful.** PT title on the left with one pill (Section 1, "Not started"), the intro panel on the right. Looks like LawHub; the left side is mostly empty with one section.
- **C. Both halves, and the other loaded sections of that PT are clickable too**, so the assignment is really "open this PrepTest, do Section 1".

Never mention, offer, or ask about Logic Games / Analytical Reasoning / "LG". The current LSAT has two section types only: Logical Reasoning (LR) and Reading Comprehension (RC).

**Why:** Joshua said so on 2026-09-11 after being asked whether a feature should cover LG: "never ever mention LG logic games. That doesn't exist on the LSAT." Bringing it up reads as not knowing the test he teaches.

**How to apply:** When enumerating section types, say "LR and RC". Never add an LG option to a question, a UI, a schema, or copy. Related: [[no-door-metaphor-in-student-copy]].
EOF

**Q12 — Can she open an assigned section more than once?**

After she finishes and does blind review, the assignment is completed. Suppose she clicks it again a week later.

- **A. Read-only: she sees her results and blind review, no retake (suggested).** The attempt is the record you review with her; re-sitting the same section would inflate the score you see. If you want her to redo it, you assign it again and it's a new attempt.
- **B. She can retake freely**, and every attempt is kept, the way Read, Recall, Colour keeps attempts.
- **C. Retake allowed, but only the first attempt counts** as the assignment's result.

if (error) throw error;
const josh = "d763bfe6-d710-4c4b-90ff-4b3a20929211";
const a = await supabase.from("homework_assignments").select("id,set_title,status").eq("student_id", josh);
console.log("homework_assignments on josh:", a.data);
const ids = (a.data ?? []).map(r => r.id);
const ci = await supabase.from("classroom_items").delete().in("source_id", ids).select("id,title");
console.log("classroom_items deleted:", ci.data?.length, ci.error);
const ha = await supabase.from("homework_assignments").delete().in("id", ids).select("id");
console.log("homework_assignments deleted:", ha.data?.length, ha.error);
const n = await supabase.from("notifications").delete().eq("user_id", josh).ilike("title", "%PT1%").select("id");
console.log("notifications deleted:", n.data?.length, n.error);
process.exit(0);
EOF

[external_agent_tool_result]
  /** The table `refId` points into, so a classroom_items row can be matched to it. */
  sourceTable: string;
  title: string;
  subtitle: string;
  status: 'assigned' | 'in_progress' | 'completed';
  createdAt: string;
  /** Due date, when a classroom_items row carries one. */
  dueAt: string | null;
  route: string;
  /**
   * Work that reports its own completion, so the student is never asked to
   * assert it. A timed section is the case: sitting it IS finishing it, and a
   * "Mark done" button beside one can be pressed without doing the section at
   * all, which is worse than useless on work assigned as a measurement.
   */
  selfCompleting?: boolean;
  /**
   * Present only on a timed section. Home opens these instructions over the
   * stage instead of navigating, so declining costs the student nothing.
   */
  briefing?: SectionBriefing;
}

type GuidedRow = GuidedDrillAssignment & { guided_drill: GuidedDrill };

function normalizeStatus(raw: string | null | undefined): AssignedTodoRow['status'] {
      // reappearing on Home.
      const itemStatus = normalizeStatus(item.status);
      const status =
        itemStatus === 'completed' || existing?.status === 'completed' ? 'completed' : itemStatus;
      byIdentity.set(`${sourceTable}:${refId}`, {
        key: `ci-${item.id}`,
        kind,
        refId,
        sourceTable,
        title: item.title || existing?.title || 'Assigned work',
        // The item carries no question count, so keep the source row's subtitle
        // when there is one.
        subtitle: existing?.subtitle ?? item.description ?? '',
        status,
        createdAt: item.created_at,
        dueAt: item.due_at,
        route,
        selfCompleting:
          (item.metadata_json as { kind?: string } | null)?.kind === 'timed_section',
        briefing: readSectionBriefing(item.metadata_json) ?? undefined,
      });
    }

    // Open work first, then completed; newest within each bucket.
    return [...byIdentity.values()].sort((x, y) => {
      const s = (x.status === 'completed' ? 1 : 0) - (y.status === 'completed' ? 1 : 0);
      if (s !== 0) return s;
      return new Date(y.createdAt).getTime() - new Date(x.createdAt).getTime();
    });
  }
}

[external_agent_tool_result]
/**
 * A timed section carries everything its briefing needs on the classroom item
 * itself. Reading it here means Home can raise the instructions in place rather
 * than sending the student to a page whose only job was to hold them.
 *
 * The authored `content_html` on the item stays where it is: it is what the
 * emailed copy and the direct link render. This is the same assignment said in
 * the fewest words that still bind, for the moment just before the clock.
 */
export interface SectionBriefing {
  /** Minutes on the clock. Accommodated timings land here exactly. */
  minutes: number;
  /** "Logical Reasoning", "Reading Comprehension", "Logic Games". */
  sectionName: string;
  /** Where Enter goes. Already carries the sealed flag and the return path. */
  href: string;
  /** Sealed runs withhold every answer and explanation for the whole sitting. */
  sealed: boolean;
}

const SECTION_NAMES: Record<string, string> = {
  lr: "Logical Reasoning",
  rc: "Reading Comprehension",
  lg: "Logic Games",
};

/**
 * Null for anything that is not a timed section, which is most work. Callers
 * treat null as "this opens the ordinary way", so a malformed row degrades to
 * the old navigation rather than to a briefing with holes in it.
 */
export function readSectionBriefing(metadata: unknown): SectionBriefing | null {
  if (!metadata || typeof metadata !== "object") return null;
  const meta = metadata as Record<string, unknown>;
  if (meta.kind !== "timed_section") return null;

  const minutes = typeof meta.minutes === "number" ? meta.minutes : null;
  const href = typeof meta.href === "string" ? meta.href : null;
  if (!minutes || minutes <= 0 || !href) return null;

  const sectionKind = typeof meta.section_kind === "string" ? meta.section_kind : "";
  return {
    minutes,
    sectionName: SECTION_NAMES[sectionKind] ?? "Timed Section",
    href,
    sealed: meta.sealed === true,
  };
}

import * as React from "react";
import type { SectionBriefing as SectionBriefingData } from "@/lib/sectionBriefing";
/**
 * The last screen before the clock.
 *
 * It covers the stage rather than replacing the page: the student stays on
 * Home, the rail and the bento go quiet underneath, and nothing about the
 * session is committed until Enter. Pressing Escape or "Not now" puts Home
 * back exactly as it was, which is the point of not navigating.
 *
 * The copy is deliberately short. This is read in the thirty seconds before a
 * timed section, standing up, and every line that is not a condition of
 * sitting it well is a line that costs attention it needs.
 */
export function SectionBriefing({
  briefing,
  onStart,
  onDismiss,
}: {
  briefing: SectionBriefingData;
  onStart: () => void;
  onDismiss: () => void;
}) {
  const startRef = React.useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    startRef.current?.focus();
  }, []);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if (e.key === "Escape") onDismiss();
    };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [onDismiss]);
  // The page behind must not scroll while the briefing is up.
  React.useEffect(() => {
    const previous = document.body.style.overflow;
    document.body.style.overflow = "hidden";
    return () => {
      document.body.style.overflow = previous;
    };
  }, []);
  return (
    <div
      className="section-briefing"
      role="dialog"
      aria-modal="true"
      aria-labelledby="section-briefing-title"
    >
      <div className="section-briefing-inner">
        <header className="section-briefing-head">
          <span className="section-briefing-eyebrow">Timed section</span>
          <h1 id="section-briefing-title">{briefing.sectionName}</h1>
          <p className="section-briefing-lede">
            {briefing.minutes} minutes, one sitting. The clock starts when you press Enter

export function primaryLabelFor(kind: HomeKind, started: boolean): string {
  const pair = PRIMARY_LABEL[kind];
  return started ? pair.resume : pair.start;
}

/**
 * The hero shows its kind as a tag, so the meta line beneath must not open by
 * saying it again. Falls back to the whole line when the kind is not the lead.
 */
export function stripKind(meta: string, kind: HomeKind): string {
  const parts = meta.split(" · ");
  if (parts.length > 1 && parts[0] === KIND_LABEL[kind]) return parts.slice(1).join(" · ");
  return meta;
}

## assistant

Found it. Here's why each button is there.

**"Open the set" vs "Open assigned work"**

They're not two ways to open the same thing. "Open the set" opens this assignment. "Open assigned work" is hard-coded on every hero card ([ClassroomHomeView.tsx:290](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx:290)) and just switches to the Assigned-work list, the column you saw in the video. It made some sense when the hero was one of several; with one section assigned it reads as a duplicate. It should go, or move to the list heading.

**"Mark done"**

The app already has the rule you want, it just isn't being applied to what my script creates. The home feed hides Mark done for any classroom item whose metadata says `kind: "timed_section"` ([useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:28](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.ts:28)), with this comment in the code: *sitting it IS finishing it, and a "Mark done" button beside one can be pressed without doing the section at all.* Those items also close themselves when the section run finishes ([Drill.tsx:2633](projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch/src/pages/Drill.tsx:2633) → `closeOutAssignedSection`).

My `--section` path writes the assignment through `homework_sets`, which stamps the item as plain `homework`, so the feed treats it like a marked-by-hand set: Mark done shows, and finishing the section doesn't close the card.

There are actually two half-built section paths in the app:

| | `timed_section` item (older) | `homework_sets` path (what I wired) |
|---|---|---|
| Home button | opens an in-place "briefing" overlay, Enter starts the clock | "Open the set" → detail page → Start |
| Mark done | hidden | shown |
| Completes itself | yes, when the run finishes | no |
| Timed launch | yes | yes (since #113) |
| Anything writes it today | no, nothing in the codebase creates one | `scripts/assign.ts` |

So the fix is to converge them: a single-section assignment should carry the `timed_section` metadata (pt, section, kind, minutes) so it inherits the no-Mark-done rule and the self-close, and the existing briefing overlay gets replaced by the full-screen LawHub directions card from the grill. That also gives the card its minutes field, which is where the accommodated time will land.

I've added this to the capture. Want me to start building from it now (one PR: metadata + directions card + hide the second button + no Mark done), or finish the last grill question first?

        subtitle: `${a.question_qids?.length ?? 0} questions${a.score != null ? ` · ${Math.round(a.score)}%` : ''}`,
        status: normalizeStatus(a.status),
        createdAt: a.created_at,
        dueAt: null,
        route: `/classroom/${encodeURIComponent(a.id)}`,

    for (const a of ta) {

        key: `ta-${a.id}`,

        title: a.title || 'From Joshua',

        status: a.status === 'viewed' ? 'in_progress' : normalizeStatus(a.status),

def patch(p, pairs):
    s=open(p,encoding='utf8').read()
    for old,new in pairs:
        assert old in s, (p, old[:70])
        s=s.replace(old,new,1)
    open(p,'w',encoding='utf8',newline='\n').write(s)

 # state

  // An assigned section may be paused, but the first press in a sitting is
  // met with the truth about the real test before it goes through. Once
  // acknowledged it is not asked again in that sitting.
  const [pauseCautionOpen, setPauseCautionOpen] = React.useState(false);

describe('an assigned PrepTest section is sat, not marked', () => {
  const sectionHw = {
    id: 'hw-9',
    set_title: 'PT156.S4 (LR)',
    question_qids: Array.from({ length: 26 }, (_, i) => `PT156-S4-Q${i + 1}`),
    status: 'assigned',
    created_at: '2026-09-11T00:00:00Z',
    score: null,
  };
  const mixedHw = { ...sectionHw, id: 'hw-10', question_qids: ['PT156-S4-Q1', 'PT101-S2-Q3'] };

  it('opens on the directions page and completes itself', () => {
    // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
    const [row] = buildAssignedRows({ ...EMPTY, hw: [sectionHw as any] });
    expect(row.route).toBe('/section/hw-9');
    expect(row.selfCompleting).toBe(true);
  });

  it('keeps the classroom page and Mark done for a mixed set', () => {
    // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
    const [row] = buildAssignedRows({ ...EMPTY, hw: [mixedHw as any] });
    expect(row.route).toBe('/classroom/hw-10');
    expect(row.selfCompleting).toBeFalsy();
  });

  it('survives the classroom_items mirror written with the old route', () => {
    const rows = buildAssignedRows({
      ...EMPTY,
      // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
      hw: [sectionHw as any],
      items: [
        item({
          id: 'ci-9',
          type: 'homework',
          title: 'PT156.S4 (LR)',
          source_table: 'homework_assignments',
          source_id: 'hw-9',
          metadata_json: { assigned_route: '/classroom/hw-9' },
        }),
      ],
    });
    expect(rows).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(rows[0].route).toBe('/section/hw-9');
    expect(rows[0].selfCompleting).toBe(true);
  });
});
EOF

description: Commit, push, open the PR
command: git add src/App.tsx src/components/classroom/ClassroomRouteViews.tsx src/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHomeView.tsx src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.ts src/hooks/useAssignedTodoFeed.test.ts src/lib/drillLaunch.ts src/lib/drillRunSpec.ts src/lib/drillRunSpec.test.ts src/lib/sectionDirections.ts src/pages/Drill.tsx src/pages/SectionDirections.tsx src/pages/SectionDirections.test.tsx src/pages/section-directions.css && git commit -q -m "Assigned PrepTest section opens on a LawHub-style directions page

An assignment whose questions are exactly one PT section now opens at
/section/:id: section number, total time, question count, the official
directions, GO TO QUESTIONS. Full-screen, untimed; the clock and the
in_progress stamp start on the button. The classroom detail page sends
such assignments there, so emailed links still work.

Home no longer offers Mark done for these (sitting the section is the
completion, which Drill already writes) and drops the duplicate 'Open
assigned work' button from the hero. After the section, blind review
opens with every question available instead of only when something was
flagged. The first Pause in an assigned section asks 'Are you sure?' and
says the real test does not allow it; later pauses in that sitting do
not re-ask.

Built, tested, and pushed as [PR #121](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/121). To answer the "why": nothing was wrong with the assignment, the directions page simply did not exist yet. It does now.

**What the branch does**

- "Open the set" (and "Open" in the list, and the emailed `/classroom/…` link) for a one-section assignment now lands on `/section/:id`: white card on the blue-grey field, "Section 4", "Total Section Time - 35 minutes", "26 Questions", the official LR directions, and the orange GO TO QUESTIONS button. Full screen, no sidebar.
- The card is untimed. GO TO QUESTIONS starts the clock and the assignment.
- Mark done is gone for section assignments; finishing the section completes it (the app already wrote that, it just wasn't being applied). The duplicate "Open assigned work" button is gone from the hero.
- After the last question: score guess, then blind review opens with every question available, then results.
- First Pause in an assigned section asks "Pause the section?" with the note that the real test does not allow it; after that it pauses freely for the rest of the sitting.
- The card and the clock read the student's timing mode (35 / 52:30 / 70 / untimed).

**Verified:** typecheck clean; 9 new tests plus the existing 131 in the touched suites pass. Not yet verified with eyes, because the Browser pane needs a signed-in student and I don't enter passwords.

[external_agent_tool_result]
// Surfaces that own the whole screen and must stay free of floating chrome.
// The exam players are the strict case: nothing may sit over the passage, the
// choices or the footer's primary action.
const OVERLAY_FREE_PREFIXES = ["/book/", "/classroom", "/drill", "/rc"];

function GlobalOverlayMount() {
  const { pathname } = useLocation();
  if (OVERLAY_FREE_PREFIXES.some((p) => pathname === p || pathname.startsWith(p))) return null;
  return (
    <>
      <FloatingHelpButton />
    </>
  );
}

// Param/query-preserving redirects for the teacher IA rebuild (ADR 0001).
// The old surfaces still resolve; they just forward to their new home so no
// bookmarked /teacher/* URL 404s.
function RedirectBootcampToInsights() {
  const { topicId } = useParams();
  return <Navigate to={`/teacher/insights/${topicId}`} replace />;
}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

    (mode && mode !== 'type-drill') ||
    !!sealed ||
    !!isGuidedDrill ||
    Object.values(rest).some((v) => v !== undefined);
  if (!carriesSomething) return undefined;
  return {
    mode: mode ?? 'type-drill',
    sealed: !!sealed,
    isGuidedDrill: !!isGuidedDrill,
    ...rest,
  };
}

  // The buzzer. Stops the clock, records where the student had got to, and
  // asks — once. A section that has already been carried past its buzzer must
  // not be asked again on every tick.
  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!timer || !session) return;
    if (session.mode !== 'full-section') return;
    if (timer.mode !== 'countdown' || timer.ms > 0) return;
    if (overtimeState !== 'none' || session.regulationMark) return;

    const answeredQids = Array.from(session.attempts.entries())
      .filter(([, a]) => !!a.selectedAnswer)
      .map(([qid]) => qid);
    setSession({ ...session, regulationMark: { answeredQids } });
    timer.pause();
    setOvertimeState('prompt');
  }, [timer, session, overtimeState]);

  // Metadata for the drawer's Details tab, drawn from the active question.

[external_agent_tool_result]
export interface DrillExplanationContext {
  data?: LrExplanationData | null;
  isLoading?: boolean;
  isError?: boolean;
  /** Nothing is shown until the answer is revealed. */
  revealed: boolean;
  /** Shared with the inline layer, so switching style swaps both surfaces. */
  activeStyle: LrStyleKey | null;
  onStyleChange: (style: LrStyleKey) => void;
  /** Hand-off to the AI tutor when the source published no prose. */
  onAskTutor?: () => void;
}

export default function DrillExplanationTab({
  data,
  isLoading,
  isError,
  revealed,
  activeStyle,
  onStyleChange,
  onAskTutor,
}: DrillExplanationContext) {
  const styles = data?.styles ?? [];
  const current = styles.find((s) => s.style === activeStyle) ?? styles[0];

  const standardSteps = styles.find((s) => s.style === "standard")?.steps ?? [];
  const borrowsStandard =
    current != null &&
    current.style !== "standard" &&
    current.steps.length === 0 &&
    standardSteps.length > 0;
  const steps = borrowsStandard ? standardSteps : current?.steps ?? [];

  if (!revealed) {
    return (
      <Body>
        <p className="text-[13px] text-slate-400">
          The explanation unlocks once you have answered or revealed this question.
        </p>
      </Body>
    );
  }
[/external_agent_tool_result]

All five are in and pushed to [PR #121](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/121). What I verified in the pane, on the josh account:

**1. Clock was stuck at 34:59.** Real bug, dev-build only. The clock started, but React's development double-mount cleared the 250 ms repaint, so the number never changed. Fixed in `useTimer`; the pane now shows it counting (27:49 → 27:44 over five seconds), and it correctly credited the minutes already spent on this sitting. Production never double-mounts, so the live site was counting down; you just couldn't see it here.

**2. Time's up.** For an assigned section the buzzer dialog now reads "Time's up." with a single button, "Go to blind review". No "Keep working". Self-run sections keep the old choice. Not exercised live (that's a 35-minute wait); covered by reading the code path, which is the same dialog that already fires.

**3. No answers during an assigned section.** Show Answer and Explanation are gone from the top strip; the drawer's Explanation tab says "Explanations open after the section is scored." Sealed is the default for every assigned section. Unsealing exists only as a launch flag (`reveal=1`) so you can ask for it at assign time later; the assign script doesn't expose it yet.

**4. Directions button.** It was showing the old paper-test text ("blacken the corresponding space on your answer sheet") in the app's dark dialog. Now it's the same digital wording as the card, white, matching the player.

**5. Pause notice.** Every section and practice test, not just assigned ones: white "Notice" card, LawHub's wording, Cancel / Ok, "Don't show me this message again". Verified live: Cancel kept the clock running; Ok paused; ticked the box, resumed, pressed Pause again, no notice; it holds for that sitting only.

On `/grillwithdocs`: there's no skill by that exact name here. The nearest is `grill-with-docs-codex`, which runs a requirements interview and then a Codex plan review. Your requirements were concrete, so I built them rather than blocking; they're logged in the capture as Q15. If you want the Codex review pass on PR #121, say so.
